22,2026-09-17,"You're viewing the US website. Looking for the Site edition?
Alec Litowitz says Citadel founder Ken Griffin appreciated ‘how his brain worked.’
Copied to clipboard
Alec Litowitz arrived at investment firm Citadel in 1994 with minimal prior experience. He lacked a traditional investing background, and his Bachelor’s degree from MIT was in Mathematics and Anthropology, not Finance. 
However, his business partner Ken Griffin saw something else, something that made him confident that Litowitz would thrive as one of Citadel’s four founding partners: how Litowitz thought. 
“It was how my brain worked that he was interested in,” Litowitz tells Entrepreneur in a new interview.
Over the next decade, Griffin’s bet paid off. Litowitz helped build Citadel’s equity businesses as the firm expanded from roughly $100 million in assets under management to $10 billion in 2003. Citadel grew from about seven employees to 750. Then, Litowitz spent 18 years leading another firm called Magnetar Capital, which he says grew to manage about $20 billion by the time he left in 2022.
His central lesson from decades spent building investment firms is about how people respond when their assumptions stop matching reality.
He calls the mindset adaptability quotient, or AQ: the capacity to recognize that your mental model is outdated, reshape it and act before circumstances force your hand.
“It is the ability to notice when your map is off of reality and go through a process to redraw it,” Litowitz says. “So that when you act, you’re moving the ball forward.”
In a world where AI and other technologies are changing jobs, industries and institutions quickly, Litowitz believes everyone must have a strong AQ. “Everybody’s an entrepreneur when the world changes this fast,” he says.
Litowitz recently wrote a book about AQ called The Adaptability Quotient: Rewiring Your Mind for Success in the Next Human Era. In it, he identifies three behaviors that signal high AQ: metacognition, simulation and experimentation.
Metacognition means thinking about your own thinking. Founders must recognize that their beliefs are shaped by personal experiences, biases and blind spots. Before assuming an apparent market gap is an opportunity, they should ask why that gap exists.
Litowitz illustrates the point with a hypothetical entrepreneur who notices a town without an ice cream shop and decides to open one. The absence of a competitor may be an opportunity, or it may reveal that residents do not want ice cream, the seasonal demand is too weak, or a related concept would work better.
“The metacognition would sit and say, Well, why isn’t there an ice cream store?” he says.
Simulation is the second step: Generate multiple explanations and possible solutions rather than falling in love with your first idea. Perhaps customers want gelato instead of ice cream. Maybe a bakery or coffee business would be a stronger concept. Maybe the better test is a pop-up rather than a permanent store.
Finally, experimentation means seeking feedback from reality cheaply and quickly. Offer desserts through a local restaurant. Run a stand at a farmers market. Test demand before signing a lease, hiring staff and spending heavily on buildout.
“How can I go test my theory without going all-in and building the building and renting the space before I know anything?” Litowitz asks.
Entrepreneurs cannot become attached to an initial answer. Their job is not to prove themselves correct; it is to discover what is true.
“You don’t get to choose reality,” Litowitz says. “Reality tells you what’s right.”
When hiring employees, he suggests asking candidates one revealing question: “Would you rather be right for the wrong reason or wrong for the right reason?” 
His preferred answer is clear. Being right for the wrong reason may create a short-term win, but it is difficult to repeat. A sound process that produces an unexpected outcome is more valuable because it can be studied, improved and used again.
“Lucky is not a business,” Litowitz says. “I want to have a process that continually creates something successful.”
Litowitz is now the founder and managing partner of investment firm Qstar, which he founded in 2017.
He noticed that the world was changing quickly around him. “The center of this dislocation is not in energy or finance,” he says. “It’s in the middle of society. This one’s affecting everybody.”
His answer is AQ. In his formulation, IQ helps people solve problems, and EQ helps them work with others. But AQ determines whether a person is asking the right question before attempting to solve it.
“AQ is the ability to sit and go, My model is no longer reality,” Litowitz says. “I can update my model and implement the new model before reality forces it on me.”
Alec Litowitz arrived at investment firm Citadel in 1994 with minimal prior experience. He lacked a traditional investing background, and his Bachelor’s degree from MIT was in Mathematics and Anthropology, not Finance. 
However, his business partner Ken Griffin saw something else, something that made him confident that Litowitz would thrive as one of Citadel’s four founding partners: how Litowitz thought. 
“It was how my brain worked that he was interested in,” Litowitz tells Entrepreneur in a new interview.

            The Federal Reserve’s first rate hike in three years has ushered in a new twist to the already unusual relationship between President Donald Trump and the world’s most powerful central bank.
    

            Trump raised eyebrows on Wednesday evening by appearing to suggest that he gave Kevin Warsh, his handpicked Fed chairman, permission to raise rates – even though it was the “wrong” move. The president implied that Warsh had no choice but to embrace a rate increase because the Fed chief was outnumbered on the “hostile” rate-setting committee he oversees.
    

            “I talked to Kevin and I said you might as well vote with the board because it’s not going to matter…I said, ‘Do what you want because it doesn’t matter’ because he doesn’t have the votes,” Trump told reporters.
    

            The remarks raise plenty of questions about how much – if at all – Warsh is consulting with a president who has been willing to cross red lines in his quest for dramatically lower interest rates.
    

            Those questions are especially sensitive because the Fed is designed to be insulated from the political winds in Washington. Central bankers are required to make trillion-dollar decisions on whether to raise or lower interest rates based on what’s best for the economy, not for the political party in power.
    

            Tellingly, some Fed watchers and former officials reacted to the remarks with disbelief.
    

            “It’s an unusual and unfortunate comment to make. Clearly, the Fed chair does not need the president’s permission to raise rates,” Eric Rosengren, former president of the Federal Reserve Bank of Boston, told CNN on Thursday. “The implication is that the chair got a hall pass. I have no idea how truthful the comment was.”
    

            Krishna Guha, vice chairman and head of economics and central bank strategy at Evercore ISI who criticized Warsh’s initial stumbles, dismissed Trump’s claims.
    

            “The market believes – and with good grounds – that Kevin Warsh owned this decision and that the committee was strongly united behind him,” Guha said. “There is no evidence here or to the market at large that Warsh was dragged into this by colleagues – or permissioned by the president.”
    

            The risk is that Trump fuels suspicions that swirled on Wall Street this summer that Warsh may not be truly independent. That’s dangerous because a Fed doing the White House’s bidding could lose control of market-set interest rates, dramatically increasing the cost of mortgages and car loans. And a loss of faith in Fed independence could unhinge inflation expectations among investors and consumers.
    

            The Fed declined to comment to CNN about Trump’s remarks.
    

            For his part, Warsh tap-danced around questions related to the man who nominated him.
    

            “I’m not a Wall Street newsletter. Part of the independence of the Federal Reserve is we stay in our lane. Independence is a two-way street,” Warsh said during the press conference on Wednesday. “We’ll let people that do trade policy and fiscal policy stay in their lane, too. That’s how we can stand up here and call them the way we see them.”
    

            Asked by a reporter when he last spoke with the president, Warsh said: “I don’t have anything for you on discussions with the president.”
    

            Tim Mahedy, CEO and chief economist at research firm Access/Macro, said he found that response from Warsh “strange.”
    

            “The right answer is, ‘I didn’t talk to the president.’ I don’t think the president is talking to Warsh beforehand. That would be shocking,” said Mahedy, a former official at the IMF and San Francisco Fed.
    

            The other risk is that, intentionally or not, Trump diminishes Warsh as an ineffectual leader – just as he appeared to be starting to find his footing.
    

            “It makes it seem like Warsh has no control over this committee, like he was completely ineffectual in convincing his colleagues,” said Mahedy.
    

            Warsh emerged from the meeting looking independent. Not only did he preside over a rate hike the White House did not want, but he explained it clearly and with the unanimous support of his colleagues – including former Fed Chair Jerome Powell.
    

            “It felt like the day the Fed became Warsh’s Fed,” Evercore’s Guha said. “He was a lot more assured, coherent and credible yesterday. What he’s saying basically adds up.”
    

            But now if Warsh and a majority of his colleagues on the committee decide against a rate hike, he will face questions about whether the president put his finger on the scale.
    

            In the past, most presidents have gone out of their way to even avoid the appearance of interfering with the Fed because it was widely accepted that an independent Fed builds confidence.
    

            None of this is to say Trump unleashed on the Fed in a way that would have unnerved investors. In fact, Trump’s initial reaction to the first rate hike under Warsh was relatively mild, especially compared with his relentless and deeply personal condemnation of Powell, his last handpicked Fed chair.
    

            While Trump bashed Warsh’s colleagues as “very hostile” and “very political,” he singled out the Fed chief for praise. “He’s a good man, Kevin Warsh,” Trump said.
    

            “People were fearful of a fierce backlash from the president against this decision to raise rates and a resumption of the conflict between the White House and the Fed,” Guha said.
    

            Of course, the Warsh-Trump relationship will continue to evolve, likely guided by what happens next to inflation, borrowing costs and the path of the S&P 500 and all-important Treasury rates.
    

            Mahedy said he worries that the relationship will break down if the Warsh-led Fed continues to raise rates – as many suspect it will be forced to.
    

            “It’s not hard to see more blame being put on the Fed and Warsh – and then it will start to look a lot like Powell,” said Mahedy. “It’s kind of a train wreck – but a slow-moving one.”

24,2026-09-17,"It feels like we've been writing HDMI 2.1 explainers, roundups, and how-to guides for years now, and the next generation is fast approaching. The HDMI 2.2 standard has been alluded to for a while, but now we're finally getting eyes on cables and pricing. The first brand to release a cable is Silkland, with its H96 Certified Ultra96 HDMI Cable, which is available in 6.6-foot and 10-foot lengths that retail for $33 and $56, respectively. Preorders are live, and shipments are expected to go out the week of October 5. Eleson is another HDMI manufacturer that plans on having HDMI 2.2 cables on shelves by mid-November.
What are the biggest HDMI evolutions with the 2.2 standard? One feature is Fixed Rate Link (FRL) technology, which allows Ultra96 cables — another term for HDMI 2.2 wires — to deliver a maximum of 96 Gbps. HDMI 2.1 was capped at 48 Gbps, and that standard already supported tech like Variable Refresh Rate, Auto Low Latency Mode, and Quick Media Switching.
However, the Ultra96 standard supports 4K resolution at up to 240Hz and 8K/60Hz, both at 10- and 12-bit color, on top of 10K, 12K, and 16K resolution support. The HDMI Forum believes that the Ultra96 name should be used by manufacturers who want to indicate that a cable or device is HDMI 2.2-certified. Interestingly, 96 Gbps is the 2.2 cutoff, but you'll also be able to purchase 64 Gbps and 80 Gbps cables that fall under the 2.2 umbrella.
According to the HDMI Forum, the organization is expecting device manufacturers to start implementing HDMI 2.2 ports in 2027. At this time, we're not sure which TV, projector, and AV receiver brands will rush 2.2 tech to market first; we're also willing to bet the actual rollout will be rather slow. Fortunately, if you're still dying to get your hands on a sleek Ultra96 cable, it'll be fully backward-compatible with older HDMI ports.
Considering the fact that many TVs are still sold with HDMI 2.0b connections (the predecessor to HDMI 2.1), personally, we wouldn't rush to outfit your home theater or gaming rig with these newer wires. Still, it's nice to see that we're gearing up for a whole new generation of HDMI bravura. Here's hoping that we don't start running into fake HDMI 2.2 cables and ports; it's an HDMI 2.1 debacle we're still trying to wrap our heads around."
25,2026-09-17,"If you own a Jaguar XJ220 or the rarer XJR-15, you’re probably not short on money or real estate. You may, however, be finding it hard to lay your hands on a diagnostic tool that is compatible with the ancient Zytek ECU. Thankfully, [Tom Radom], is working to change that. 
As the owner of XJ220 chassis #50, [Tom] was unlucky enough to see the dreaded check engine light come on last year. He was lucky enough to get his hands on an SPD-1039 interface, built to talk to the Zytek ECU that runs the car. However, he was painfully aware that these devices are now incredibly rare and hard to come by, even given the tiny limited market of owners of 1990s Jaguar supercars. Thus, he set about building a replacement.
An initial effort to open up and reverse engineer the Zytek interface failed, mostly because it had been potted rather successfully in silicone. Thus, [Tom] took a new tack. He hooked up a logic analyser between the interface and the car, recording the traffic between the two. This captured data allowed [Tom] to recreate the interface using a FT232RNL bridge to chat to the Zytek engine control unit. There was no attempt to rebuild the software—the original DOS-based diagnostic still runs perfectly fine in an emulator. It was just the hardware interface from a serial port to the car that needed to be recreated.
[Tom] has been building the modules and supplying them to owners of XJ220, XJ220S, and XJR-15 models at no charge. He believes simply having a viable diagnostic tool available will increase the value of these classic cars by more than the costs he incurred to develop the interface. Interested parties can submit their details via webform on Port220.com.
For a modern vehicle, the ECU is everything. Without it, the car doesn’t run. That’s why we’ve seen some heroic fixes to rescue cars and get them back on the road. If you’re doing your own wizardry to talk to cars of the past, don’t hesitate to notify the tipsline.

Tech > Services & Software > Applications
Uber Eats also expanded its Costco delivery service from 17 states to 47.
September 17, 2026, 5:38 pm ET
Ready to get your 1-gallon jug of ketchup and 12-month supply of toilet paper delivered to your door? 
Starting Thursday, DoorDash will offer same-day delivery from every Costco warehouse in the US, the online delivery service said. The announcement comes a day after Uber Eats said it was expanding its Costco delivery from 17 states to 47. DoorDash customers need to have a Costco membership, which costs $65 for the Gold Star plan and $135 for the Executive plan. 
To get started in the DoorDash app, search for Costco and select your local warehouse. Then link the Costco membership and complete the order. The process is basically the same for Uber Eats users.
DoorDash said “groceries, pantry staples, household essentials, electronics, seasonal merchandise, Kirkland Signature products and more” will be available for delivery. Delivery fees will vary, depending on the order, and DashPass members will get free delivery on eligible orders.
Costco has had a delivery partnership with Instacart since 2013. Instacart users don’t need a Costco membership, though they’ll likely pay higher prices than members.
Read more: The 9 Best Grocery Delivery Services of 2026
DoorDash was already delivering Costco products in Australia, New Zealand, Sweden, Iceland and Puerto Rico, and the company added the US to the list after seeing that “Costco” was among the most-searched retailers by app users.
Online grocery delivery has been growing fast in recent years. Peapod, HomeGrocer and Webvan tried and failed in the early years of the dot-com boom, partly because of slow internet speeds and high infrastructure costs. But the COVID pandemic triggered a massive surge in the e-grocery business. 
After the nationwide lockdown started in early 2020, monthly online grocery sales in the US jumped to $6.5 billion, up from $2 billion before the pandemic. More than 57% of US households bought groceries online, compared to 25% prior to the lockdown.
Though many returned to brick-and-mortar stores, online grocery shopping has started to pick up again, thanks to deliveries that are faster and more streamlined than ever. Some items from Amazon come in 30 minutes or less, and Walmart also offers half-hour delivery in several US markets. Gopuff delivers convenience items such as snacks, drinks and cleaning products, with thousands in as fast as 15 minutes.
Online grocery sales increased more than 20% year over year for six consecutive quarters through March of this year.
Many US households are also turning more to Costco (and Walmart) as prices continue to rise for groceries, electronics, gas and pretty much everything else. Market research firm Placer.ai said trips to Costco are up by more than 18% since 2019, and Consumer Reports rated Costco and Walmart as offering the best value for groceries.
The DoorDash-Costco partnership is a great new opportunity for delivery workers, but both companies have to make it a win-win for drivers, says Peter Schlactus, co-founder and executive director of the Association for Delivery Drivers. A4DD provides safety training, discounted gear and insurance, and job opportunities for members.
“DoorDash and Costco should together ensure that driver treatment and working conditions meet the highest standards, enabling dedicated drivers to support themselves comfortably as they deserve,” Schlactus told CNET. 
“In their haste to recruit drivers, many gig apps minimize what they ask of drivers and safety training is often a casualty. This puts both drivers and the public at risk, especially given the time pressures that this work entails.”
According to the company’s website, DoorDash drivers (called “Dashers”) receive 100% of all customer tips, including those earned on DoorDash Drive orders.

US may be 'forced to intervene' in the Red Sea
The United States reiterated its support for Yemen’s internationally recognised government on Wednesday as fighting intensified between the Saudi-led coalition and Houthi forces. US officials also held direct talks with Houthi representatives in Oman last weekend, according to sources cited by Reuters.
Yemeni government forces said that the coalition had struck Houthi positions around Mocha and that government forces had launched drone attacks in Marib. The Houthis claimed to have shot down a Saudi F-15 fighter jet over Marib.
Saudi Arabia is running low on missile interceptors and has asked regional and Western allies for help, according to two officials who spoke to The Associated Press news agency. The Saudis asked France, Pakistan, Egypt and the United Kingdom to deploy air-defence support to help defend against attacks by the Houthis. One official said Saudi Arabia’s main ally and arms supplier, the US, has seen its own stock of interceptors dwindle over the course of the war with Iran.
US Central Command (CENTCOM) spokesman Tim Hawkins told Al Jazeera that the US is “closely monitoring” the situation between Houthis and the Saudi-backed government in Yemen, adding that the US military has a longstanding “military relationship with our Saudi partners, and you see that manifesting today”.
 

 
Even as Washington assists the Saudi campaign, American officials held direct talks with Houthi representatives in Oman last weekend, according to three sources who spoke to the Reuters news agency on condition of anonymity. The meeting took place at the US embassy in Muscat with the assistance of the Omani government, two of the sources said.
At the Muscat meeting, the Houthis told US officials they had no intention of attacking US vessels and were committed to the 2025 ceasefire with the US, according to the sources cited by Reuters.
Meanwhile, the top US diplomat for Yemen, Neil Hop, told Yemen’s Foreign Minister Afrah al-Zouba that the US maintained its “strong support” for Yemen’s internationally recognised government, according to the US embassy in Yemen.
“The United States of America stands in solidarity with the Yemen government and its people against Iran-backed Houthi aggression,” the embassy said, citing Hop in a post on X.
The Yemeni government’s army said the Saudi-led coalition targeted Houthi outposts and equipment around the southwestern port city of Mocha. In a separate social media post, the government’s army also said they conducted drone attacks on Houthi forces in northern Marib governorate.
On Wednesday evening, Houthi military spokesman Yahya Saree said that “Saudi warplanes launched 40 air strikes targeting the provinces of Taiz, Marib and Hodeidah over the past 24 hours”. In response, the Houthis targeted the Khamis Mushait airbase and an oil facility in the Saudi Red Sea port city of Yanbu, the group said in a statement.
The Houthis also claimed to have shot down a Saudi fighter jet using a “locally made surface-to-air missile” over the central province of Marib. Al Jazeera’s Yemen correspondent Yousef Mawry said the downing of Saudi fighter jets “could very well mean a change in the balance of power” as “Saudi Arabia depends heavily on its ability to carry out air strikes against Houthi positions in various provinces”.

30,2026-09-17,"Once famous for its ties to business schools, McKinsey is casting a wider net to meet its needs.
For decades, an MBA was one of the clearest routes into elite consulting. As AI changes the work firms do for clients, the consulting industry is looking for people who can build technology as well as devise the corporate strategy around it.
""Our recruiting changed dramatically in the last few years. We recruit from places we haven't recruited before,"" Ben Ellencweig, who leads McKinsey's AI arm, QuantumBlack, told Business Insider. ""Today many, many of our colleagues are coming from non-MBA backgrounds, and then technology too is kind of the next wave.""
That includes mathematicians, computer-science PhDs, startup founders without degrees, and people with experience at technology companies.
The shift is playing out beyond McKinsey. Job postings from Deloitte, PwC, EY, KPMG, McKinsey, BCG, and Bain fell by about 24% in the year ending May 2026 compared to the year before, according to labor-market intelligence firm Draup. At the same time, AI jobs grew from 1.4% to 5% of their total openings.
The number of forward-deployed-engineer openings across the seven firms rose from none to 1,404 in that same period, according to the analysis. Such roles require engineers who can deploy and run production AI systems inside organizations, and also do the client-facing work traditionally associated with consulting.
According to McKinsey's current job postings, most of those roles require at least four or five years of computer-related engineering experience.
Ellencweig said McKinsey is looking to fill these roles with ""curious people"" who are excited to work with clients and solve problems through technology, but who also bring deep technical skills. The firm is recruiting from different geographies, schools, and ""outside-of-school, non-school backgrounds,"" he said.
In an email to Business Insider, the firm added that it is recruiting through ""less traditional pathways,"" such as coding programs and bootcamps.

QuantumBlack itself has grown from 47 data scientists working with a Formula 1 team to ""well above 5,000"" AI professionals over the past decade, Ellencweig said. Those workers include data scientists, data engineers, designers, product managers, and product owners.
McKinsey rival EY has recently spoken about its own push to widen its talent pool. Ginnie Carlier, EY Americas' chief talent and culture officer, told BI earlier this year that the Big Four firm was once exclusively made up of accountants, but now it also hires engineers, creatives, and technologists.
Ellencweig said McKinsey is reinventing itself around technology, including how it hires and how it develops current employees.
""I view our entire workforce, the entire McKinsey, including our consultant cohort, becoming very technology savvy,"" McKinsey's Ellencweig said.

31,2026-09-17,"If Canada wants to become more European, it may help to start with the continent's most beloved pastime: soccer.
Canadian Prime Minister Mark Carney and his British counterpart Andy Burnham watched Everton beat Wolverhampton Wanderers 1-0 on Wednesday. They were pictured pumping their fists, hugging, and donning scarves in Everton's colors — blue and white.
It was also a useful piece of diplomatic theater.
Everton is not a particularly flashy team. The Toffees haven't won a major trophy for 31 years, typically finish in the lower half of the Premier League, and are consistently overshadowed by their local rival, Liverpool. On the other hand, Evertonians are among the most committed and passionate fans in the sport.
Carney, who spent seven years as Bank of England governor, previously made his Everton support public while he lived in the country. So too has Burnham, a native of northwest England, whose charisma and down-to-earth everyman image was key in elevating him to prime minister in July.
A historic meeting marked with a win. 👌🔵@Andyburnham 🤝 @MarkJCarney pic.twitter.com/s4qmuAjzRJ
While Burnham, a longtime season-ticket holder, used to sit in the stands at Hill Dickinson Stadium, security requirements meant he and Carney had to watch from the corporate seats.
However, that may have been an ideal setting as they also held a bilateral meeting at the stadium amid Canada's push to build its diplomatic relationships away from the US.
Carney's trip to the soccer came just a few hours after European Commission President Ursula von der Leyen backed proposals for Canada to become an associate member of the European Union.
This would be an entirely new title, but could come with a range of benefits, from visa-free travel to access to the European single market, with its free movement of goods.

Closer trade ties to the EU — the world's second-largest economy by GDP — would be a boon for Canada as its relationship with the US falters.
The two countries have been locked in tariff disputes for months, which has even seen Donald Trump rename Lake Ontario to ""Lake America.""
Canada this month imposed new duties of up to 50% on hundreds of US imports, including cheese, honey, and aluminum foil, in retaliation for Trump's tariffs on Canadian goods.
While tensions are hot, it would be extremely challenging for Canada to extricate itself from US trade and pivot to Europe. More than three-quarters of its exports go south of the border.
Late on Wednesday, Trump threatened to retaliate against the EU if it follows through on the proposal to make Canada an associate member, calling the prospect ""laughable.""
""If they do that, if I think it's at all a hostile act, I will put very serious tariffs or stop trading with Europe on many things,"" Trump told reporters.
While the UK has been outside the EU since Brexit in 2020, Burnham has said he wants the country to be ""bolder"" in working closer with the bloc. Rejoining may be a politically fraught move, but closer trade relations aren't off the table.

30th CelebrationPokÃ©mon TCGpull ratesScalpers 
While it usually takes a good few days before we get the more official pull rate numbers for any new PokÃ©mon TCG set, early evidence is showing the 30th Celebration to be the most generous set in years. We’re talking Crown Zenith levels of good pulls, with more than one in two packs having at least an ex card, and the best rates on Special Illustration Rares of all time. And that’s despite this set having half the number of cards in each pack. As a result, for the first time in two years, it means the resale market has absolutely flattened!
The 30th Celebration follows the pattern we’re now seeing every five years, with Pikachu stamps on every card, and a combination of new cards and reprinted classics. The difference this time is that, for the first time ever, international audiences are getting these cards at the same time as Japan, with the bizarre side effect of the rest of the world also getting only five cards a pack, as is the norm in Japan. But despite half the cards from which to hit your luck, the pull rates shown by YouTubers tearing through hundreds of packs are indicating a better than one in two chance of getting an ex or better. And there’s a lot of better.
The 30th Celebration is unique in many ways. Every card in every pack is at least a holo, and every pack comes with a holo energy card, and a guaranteed holo Pikachu from a subset of 30 cards featuring the yellow mouse. There are the now-familiar ex cards, a huge range of Illustration Rares (IRs), Special Illustration Rares (SIRs), and some new Future Rare cards featuring Mew and Mewtwo. But on top of that, there’s also the Classic Collection, 30 fan-favorite cards from across the last 30 years, including the original base set holo Charizard, the Gengar Prime, up to that glorious Magikarp from Paldea Evolved. And then, on top of all that, there are three RGB Mew cards so rare that The PokÃ©mon Company won’t even acknowledge they exist (and might not for months).
When Crown Zenithâthe climactic set for the Sword & Shield eraâfirst released in January 2023, fans rejoiced. The special set had a hit rate on another level, with a 50-percent chance of finding a V card or better, and had its incredible Galarian Gallery of exceptionally beautiful cards that weren’t impossible to pull. It was so good, in fact, that it caused the opening era of Scarlet & Violet sets to feel anticlimactic and unpleasantly stingy.
In the three years since, those pull rates have only gotten more cruel, with The PokÃ©mon Company International choosing to guarantee a holo in every pack (thus making them bulk) and flooding sets with the new version of ex cards, alongside increasingly rare IRs and SIRs. The latter, the most desirable and intricately illustrated cards, have become so ridiculously hard to find that you’re unlikely to see even one in a 36-pack booster box. I believe I went three sets in a row, opening 50 packs a set, without seeing a single one. And this, of course, has only made the scalping misery that has put theÂ PTCG in a quagmire for two yearsÂ much worse.
But for now, for this set at least, that’s all changing. And it’s having the excellent effect of already seeing prices tumbling to pre-bubble levels. Even the Future Rare Mew and Mewtwo, initially being priced at around $1,000 on release day, are now selling for around $150. Why? Because people can pull them from packs! Kids and collectors are able to get the cards they want the most, and in a volume that prevents scalpers and resellers from being able to charge ludicrous amounts.
It usually takes two or three weeks from the release day for prices to sensibly level out, so we could see even these numbers come down (or, as the market figures itself out, in some cases go up). Those buying cards to gamble on riches are out of luck, and thank god.
While we need to wait a few days before the usual suspects do their full calculations on 30th Celebration to get official pull rates, the results are there to see on any number of YouTube channels.
The excruciating Leon Hart also opened 151 packs and saw very similar results, pulling both the Mew and Mewtwo Future Rares in under 50 packs. Meanwhile, pull rate expert Danny Phantump has begun his experiments and is seeing great results, with plans to reveal figures on Friday.
DigitalTQ has some early numbers too, but these are based on a far larger 420 packs. This shows the Mew and Mewtwo at an almost 1 percent chance of showing up, which is significantly higher than we were seeing for Hyper Rares in previous sets. Most striking is the frequency of SIRs, which are reported at 5.24 percent, four to five times higher than the rates seen in Scarlet & Violet and Mega Evolution sets over the last four years. SIRs have been limited for so long to around 1 in 80 packs, making them miserable to get hold of, but this has now leapt up to better than 1 in 20 packs.
Illustration Rares, meanwhile, double in frequency, appearing in one in every five packs, rather than every ten. It means if you get an Elite Trainer Box or collection box, there’s a really strong chance of getting at least one gorgeous full art card, making the hobby just so much more fun. And, equally importantly, deflating the power of scalpers and resellers.
There are still those RGB Mews in there, meaning there’s still the thrill of hunting down something extraordinarily rare for those who need it. There are no official figures on these yet, given how few have been pulled so far, but rumors suggest they could be as rare as one in every 20,000 packs.
At bloody last. While there is little reason to believe this could be the beginning of a new trend, given the pleasure of Crown Zenith was immediately shot down by Scarlet & Violet‘s sets, it’s still something to celebrate for now. And it’s a rare joy to see those resale prices plummeting.

35,2026-09-17,OpenAI is apparently still a defendant.
36,2026-09-17,"Mozilla has partnered with Mistral AI to bring the French company’s models to Smart Window, the AI browsing assistant in beta in Firefox.
The two companies announced the deal on Wednesday in a joint post, alongside the feature’s launch in France.
Smart Window, introduced by Mozilla in August, is a separate AI-assisted browsing mode. It summarises complex searches with sources, helps users find pages they clicked away from, and draws on the tabs they have open.
The two companies describe the arrangement slightly differently. Mistral’s post says Smart Window “is now powered by Mistral models”, while Mozilla’s own announcement says it is adding Mistral Small 4 as one option, and that users can still pick from a range of other models.
Smart Window was already available in English in the US and Canada, and France is its first new market, with official French-language support. Mistral said the UK and Germany are expected to follow later this year.
Mozilla said it chose Mistral Small 4 partly for its multilingual performance, and Mistral said it is fine-tuning models on regional languages and dialects.
Privacy is central to the pitch. Smart Window conversations are not saved on Mozilla’s servers by default, and model partners including Mistral agree to zero data retention, according to the announcement.
Mozilla chief executive Anthony Enzor-DeMeo cast the deal as a stand against AI gatekeepers.
“AI is becoming part of how people experience the web every day. We want to make sure that doesn’t mean people are chained to one company’s self-serving pipeline,” he said. “A browser shouldn’t be a one-way funnel.”
Arthur Mensch, Mistral’s co-founder and chief executive, called it a partnership between “two open source advocates” that brings “privacy, control and choice to AI-powered web browsing.”
For Mistral, which mostly sells to businesses and governments, the deal is a route to consumers, and it comes days after the company raised €3bn at a €21bn valuation. Mistral said the partnership lets it reach beyond enterprise customers to users worldwide.
For Mozilla, it is part of a careful push into AI. Firefox users objected to earlier AI plans, and in February the company added a single setting that blocks all of the browser’s generative AI features. Smart Window stays optional, and users can control which tabs and history it can see.

38,2026-09-17,"Amazon's stock chart tells one story while the business underneath tells a completely different one, and the gap between those two narratives is exactly where the opportunity lives.
Read More: Learn 7 secret wealth tips high net worth investors use that most investors miss (sponsor)
This post may contain links from our sponsors and affiliates, and Flywheel Publishing may receive compensation for actions taken through them.
I keep buying Amazon (NASDAQ:AMZN | AMZN Price Prediction) because the setup in front of me is the cleanest risk-reward asymmetry I own. The stock is down 5.87% over the past month and up only 6.56% year to date, while the business underneath it just posted the fastest AWS growth in 18 quarters. That gap is why I keep adding.
My thesis is simple. Amazon is spending like a company that sees demand it cannot fill, and the market is pricing it like a company whose free cash flow just went negative. Both statements are true. Only one of them matters in ten years. Trailing-twelve-month free cash flow is -$7.6 billion, and Q2 capital expenditures hit $54.2 billion. That is the near-term pain. On the other side of it sits a data-center footprint management said can be monetized for 30-plus years once online.
First, AWS is compounding into something larger. Q2 AWS revenue was $42.23 billion, up 37% year over year, with an annualized revenue run rate of $169 billion and a backlog of $496 billion, growing triple digits year over year. Andy Jassy told the Street he now believes AWS can be a “trillion dollar annual revenue business for us in time.” The 39.4% AWS operating margin tells me the growth is not being bought with giveaways.
Second, the ad business keeps quietly turning into a monster. Advertising revenue was $19.81 billion, up 26% year over year. That is a high-margin stream layered directly on top of the retail flywheel, and it flows through operating income, which grew 43.24% year over year in Q2.
Third, the balance sheet lets Amazon absorb the capex cycle without sweating. Debt-to-equity is 0.37, net debt to EBITDA is 0.45, and interest coverage is 35.17 times. Return on equity still runs 22.29%. Companies with a broken model do not post those numbers.
I own cloud peers, but my incremental dollar goes here. Microsoft (NASDAQ:MSFT) and Alphabet (NASDAQ:GOOGL) are strong hyperscaler operators, but neither has Amazon’s specific setup: an AWS unit that just accelerated for the fifth straight quarter, custom-silicon economics where Graviton offers 30% to 40% better price performance and is used by 98% of the top 1,000 EC2 customers, and an AI plus chips run rate that each eclipsed $25 billion growing triple digits. On the retail side, Walmart (NYSE:WMT) is the natural comparison, but Amazon is already the second-largest grocer in the United States with grocery gross merchandise sales exceeding $150 billion last year, and it owns AWS, ads, Prime Video, and Zoox on top of that. I am paying 20 times trailing earnings for the whole stack.
The real risk is the capex bill. 2026 capex is planned at roughly $200 billion, and prediction markets currently price a 91.5% chance capex exceeds $200 billion. If AI demand softens before those data centers monetize, free cash flow stays ugly. What keeps me buying anyway is that the “lion’s share” of 2027 capacity is largely reserved and most AI capacity is being contracted for at least five-year terms. That is contracted revenue waiting for a switch to flip, and it is also why the power, cooling, and networking suppliers behind those data centers keep showing up in our free report on seven AI infrastructure stocks that aren’t chipmakers.
I own Amazon because the market is charging me for the risk today and giving me the compounding for free. As long as AWS backlog keeps building and Jassy keeps writing checks against contracted demand, my finger stays on the button.

39,2026-09-17,"ON Semiconductor scheduled an investor day to make its case to Wall Street and watched its stock sink 9% through the presentation. Now the CEO is explaining a $30 billion gap in his own slide deck, and buyers are deciding…

ON Semiconductor (NASDAQ:ON | ON Price Prediction) hosted an investor day designed to reset the story around its power portfolio and its AI data center runway, and the shares fell through the presentation. The stock closed the regular session down 9.02% at $66.60, extending a one-month drawdown of 20.12% even as the year-to-date return remains positive at 22.99%.
CEO Hassane El-Khoury spent his CNBC hit explaining a gap in the slide deck. The total addressable market shown on stage was smaller than the one investors had been carrying in their models since June, and the growth math behind the AI data center opportunity was anchored to a consensus assumption rather than a proprietary one. Both explanations may be technically correct, yet the sellers still showed up during the event itself, which is the only question that matters when a management team schedules a day to make its case and loses ground doing it.
Hassane El-Khoury presented a total addressable market of $213 billion at the investor day, well below the $243 billion figure the company cited in June when it announced the Synaptics (NASDAQ:SYNA) acquisition.
His explanation was procedural. “Back in June, when we talked about 243, we were talking about the thesis and the strategy with the acquisition of Synaptics, according to ON Semiconductor. Today, the focus of today is core onsemi business because once you announce the deal, we’re two separate companies,” El-Khoury said.
That is how antitrust review works. Acquirer and target run independently until closing, and presenting a combined market opportunity before regulators sign off invites problems the company does not want.
The trouble is that the Synaptics transaction is expected to close in mid-2027, and a shareholder pricing the combined entity has to underwrite nine more months of separate reporting while rates and cyclicality do their own work.
El-Khoury framed the AI data center growth assumption as prudent, saying the company “talked about a 30% market, which is in the middle of the range that everybody’s talking about” and then “anchored on a 10% on top of whatever market you want to pick.”
Anchoring to the middle of the range is consensus dressed up as prudence.
The 10-point outgrowth claim is the more interesting number because it is testable only through design wins. Management already disclosed real ones, including expanded content in the NVIDIA (NASDAQ:NVDA) MGX ecosystem, two power-supply platform wins with Great Wall in China, and AWS power-supply and battery-backup content in the United States.
Until those wins translate into revenue that visibly separates ON from the market it is anchoring to, the outgrowth is an assertion, and the buy side is entitled to want more than that on a day billed as a strategic reset.
El-Khoury called the company’s 53% gross margin target a milestone on the way to a higher bar. Getting there requires the revenue mix to shift meaningfully toward silicon carbide, gallium nitride, and Treo analog content, which carry richer margin profiles than the legacy portfolio the company has been exiting.
The Q2 non-GAAP gross margin of 39.3% gives you the current baseline, and management’s own sensitivity of 25 to 30 basis points of gross-margin improvement for every point of utilization shows how much of the bridge depends on volume that has not yet arrived, disclosed in the Q2 2026 8-K.
Asked about the recent stretch, El-Khoury pivoted to the long run, saying ON “grew 72% our revenue in automotive from 2019 to 2026.” That is a fair rebuttal to an unfair quarterly comparison, and also a convenient way to avoid discussing an Analog and Mixed-Signal segment that was down 2% year over year in the most recent quarter.
The bull case is that today’s selloff has already repriced a business whose per-vehicle and per-rack content is measurably rising, and whose AI data center revenue is guided to more than double in 2026 with silicon carbide inside AI applications growing nearly 60% year over year. The forward P/E near 17x against an average analyst target of $106.88 is where that argument lives.
The bear case is that automotive semiconductor demand is cyclical, the Synaptics deal adds integration risk before it adds earnings, and a company that has to explain its own slide deck on stage is not controlling its narrative. The trailing P/E of 47x leaves little room for another miss.
The variable that decides between them is design-win disclosure. If ON can name the AI data center customers and rack content behind the 10-point outgrowth claim over the next two quarters, the investor day gets re-read as a buying opportunity. If it cannot, the anchoring language starts to sound like a placeholder.

40,2026-09-17,"Semiconductor stocks are posting a third straight session of gains, but one chip name is running so far ahead of the pack that it raises an uncomfortable question about whether the move reflects genuine conviction or something riskier.
 Market Movers desk. Editor: David Moadel.

Advanced Micro Devices (NASDAQ:AMD | AMD Price Prediction) is leading a third consecutive session of gains for semiconductor stocks, with AMD stock up 7% to $548.76 in Thursday afternoon trading. The move sits inside a broad chip bounce, with the iShares Semiconductor ETF (NASDAQ:SOXX) up 3% to $519.50. AMD stock is running well ahead of the sector benchmark that holds it.
The wider NASDAQ 100 tape is quieter today. The Invesco QQQ Trust (NASDAQ:QQQ) is up 2% to $716.41, so the day’s strength is concentrated in chips rather than large-cap tech more broadly. That backdrop matters, because the AMD move is a group story rather than a solo one.
Broadcom (NASDAQ:AVGO) stock is up 3% to $349.02, extending prior gains tied to AI accelerator momentum. Also rising, NVIDIA (NASDAQ:NVDA) stock is up 2% to $218.70, edging higher as the group’s market-cap leader while AMD carries the larger percentage gain of the three.
Today’s action in AMD reflects a semiconductor rebound reaching its third consecutive session rather than an earnings release, guidance change or analyst rating action, and AMD stock is the highest-beta way to own that bounce inside the large-cap chip group.
One same-day development at AMD is worth naming, and it explicitly isn’t the catalyst here. Advanced Micro Devices said it is working with SiFive to tune its open-source ROCm software so it runs on RISC-V datacenter servers for AI workloads. No reporting connects that announcement to the size of the price move, so the article treats it as strategy rather than trigger.
The AMD announcement fits the longer argument that the company is building a software ecosystem around its silicon, not only shipping parts. A demonstration on RISC-V servers isn’t the same as broad customer adoption, though, so the news lives closer to a directional signal than to a near-term revenue line for AMD.
AMD stock is up 156% year to date, a run that sits in a different category from its closest large-cap chip peers. Broadcom stock is up 1% over the same period, and AMD stock has outrun both its peers and the fund that holds it by a wide margin this year. NVIDIA’s move today comes off a much larger market-cap base, which is why the same tape produces a smaller percentage number there than at AMD.
That gap between AMD and the rest of the sector is the substance behind the split view of AMD stock right now. The bull case reads the year-to-date figure as the market registering share gains in accelerators and server CPUs, the one corner of semiconductors where spending keeps expanding through cycles. The bear case reads the same figure as an overextended move with further to fall if today’s group bounce fails to hold.
Both readings rest on the same fact, which is why AMD stock keeps trading with the widest daily range in the group even on days when the direction is clear. However, the plain interpretation is that AMD is the highest-beta way to own a semiconductor recovery, and that cuts in both directions rather than confirming either camp (we reverse-engineered what the biggest tech winners looked like early and put the pattern in a free playbook here).
Investors can watch for whether AMD stock holds its intraday gain into the close, since a third up session in the group is the setup the next tape will be judged against. A close near the day’s highs would extend the technical case around AMD, while a fade into the afternoon would put today’s move in a different light and reset the near-term risk view on the name.
Traders may want to keep an eye on whether the Instinct and Helios ramp AMD has flagged for the back half shows up in supply-chain checks before the next quarterly update. Their position sizing in AMD shares should reflect that the stock carries the highest beta in this large-cap chip group, so exposure should be scaled to the drawdown they can tolerate rather than to the run already booked in the year-to-date figure.
The setup for AMD stock is a rally driven by sector flows rather than a company-specific catalyst, and that’s the plain read on the day. A sector bounce that carries the group’s most-owned momentum name to a fresh price this quickly can extend, but it can also unwind on the first weak session, and the size of AMD’s year-to-date move is what makes both outcomes matter to anyone already long the name.
